The Thud game is a battle between Dwarfs and Trolls on an octagonal board. The Dwarf's goal is to maximize their score (remaining Dwarfs) minus the Troll's score (remaining Trolls *4). The game ends under certain conditions, like no pieces left or both players declaring over.

The client needs to be a JavaScript class with constructor, turn(), and end_turn() methods. The turn() function determines the Dwarf's move each turn. The end_turn() can decide if the game should be declared over.

Looking at the rules:

Dwarf Movement:
- Walk: Move any distance in a direction until blocked (other pieces, board edge), but can only kill a troll if adjacent.
- Hurl: If in a straight line (horizontal, vertical, diagonal) of N pieces, the end can be hurled N spaces. This can kill a troll if it lands on it, giving a +4 point swing.

Troll Movement:
- Walk one space, killing adjacent Dwarfs.
- Shove: If in line, can push the end N spaces towards a Dwarf-adjacent space, killing more.

The strategy for Dwarfs should focus on surviving, killing trolls when possible, and keeping as many Dwarfs on the board as possible.

First, the client's turn() function needs to choose a move. Let me think about possible strategies:

1. **Prioritize Immediate Threats**: Find moves that can eliminate a troll using hurl. Since that directly reduces the troll count, giving a +4 advantage.

2. **Protect Dwarfs from Troll Moves**: Anticipate where trolls can move next turn and avoid leaving Dwarfs in positions where they'll be killed during the troll's turn.

3. **Distribute Dwarfs to Avoid Being Shove Targets**: Trolls can shove if in a line. If Dwarfs are spread out, they can't form lines for hurl (but that might be necessary for hurling). Hmm, balance between forming lines for hurl and avoiding lines that could be exploited by trolls?

Wait, Dwarfs can form lines to hurl, but trolls also form lines to shove. We need to encourage Dwarfs to form lines so they can hurl, but trolls' lines are a threat.

So, perhaps prioritize creating lines for hurling, since that can eliminate trolls effectively.

- In each turn, first check all possible moves that result in killing a troll (hurl into them). Because killing a troll gives a big point boost. So check all possible hurl moves, select the best one.

    <#sep#>import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
from torch import nn


class AdaptiveInstanceNorm2d(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, num_features, eps=1e-05, momentum=0.1):
        super(AdaptiveInstanceNorm2d, self).__init__()
        self.num_features = num_features
        self.eps = eps
        self.momentum = momentum
        self.weight = None
        self.bias = None
        self.register_buffer('running_mean', torch.zeros(num_features))
        self.register_buffer('running_var', torch.ones(num_features))

    def forward(self, x):
        assert self.weight is not None and self.bias is not None, 'Please assign AdaIN weight first'
        b, c = x.size(0), x.size(1)
        running_mean = self.running_mean.repeat(b)
        running_var = self.running_var.repeat(b)
        x_reshaped = x.contiguous().view(1, b * c, *x.size()[2:])
        out = F.batch_norm(x_reshaped, running_mean, running_var, self.
            weight, self.bias, True, self.momentum, self.eps)
        return out.view(b, c, *x.size()[2:])

    def __repr__(self):
        return self.__class__.__name__ + '(' + str(self.num_features) + ')'


class Conv2dBlock(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, in_dim, out_dim, ks, st, padding=0, norm='none',
        activation='relu', pad_type='zero', use_bias=True, activation_first
        =False, dropout='none'):
        super(Conv2dBlock, self).__init__()
        self.use_bias = use_bias
        self.activation_first = activation_first
        if pad_type == 'reflect':
            self.pad = nn.ReflectionPad2d(padding)
        elif pad_type == 'replicate':
            self.pad = nn.ReplicationPad2d(padding)
        elif pad_type == 'zero':
            self.pad = nn.ZeroPad2d(padding)
        else:
            assert 0, 'Unsupported padding type: {}'.format(pad_type)
        norm_dim = out_dim
        if norm == 'bn':
            self.norm = nn.BatchNorm2d(norm_dim)
        elif norm == 'in':
            self.norm = nn.InstanceNorm2d(norm_dim)
        elif norm == 'adain':
            self.norm = AdaptiveInstanceNorm2d(norm_dim)
        elif norm == 'none':
            self.norm = None
        else:
            assert 0, 'Unsupported normalization: {}'.format(norm)
        if activation == 'relu':
            self.activation = nn.ReLU()
        elif activation == 'lrelu':
            self.activation = nn.LeakyReLU(0.2)
        elif activation == 'tanh':
            self.activation = nn.Tanh()
        elif activation == 'none':
            self.activation = None
        else:
            assert 0, 'Unsupported activation: {}'.format(activation)
        if dropout == 'none':
            self.dropout = None
        else:
            self.dropout = nn.Dropout(dropout)
        self.conv = nn.Conv2d(in_dim, out_dim, ks, st, bias=self.use_bias)

    def forward(self, x):
        if self.activation_first:
            if self.activation:
                x = self.activation(x)
            x = self.conv(self.pad(x))
            if self.norm:
                x = self.norm(x)
        else:
            x = self.conv(self.pad(x))
            if self.norm:
                x = self.norm(x)
            if self.activation:
                x = self.activation(x)
        if self.dropout:
            x = self.dropout(x)
        return x


def get_inputs():
    return [torch.rand([4, 4, 4, 4])]


def get_init_inputs():
    return [[], {'in_dim': 4, 'out_dim': 4, 'ks': 4, 'st': 4}]
<#sep#>2^8 = 6 \cdot 256 = 1536 \)
4. \( \binom{4}{3} \cdot 1^8 = 4 \cdot 1 = 4 \)

Substituting these values into the formula:
\[
S(8, 4) = \frac{1}{24} \left[ 65536 - 26244 + 1536 - 4 \right]
\]

Simplify inside the brackets:
\[
65536 - 26244 = 39292
\]
\[
39292 + 1536 = 40828
\]
\[
40828 - 4 = 40824
\]

Now, divide by \( 4! = 24 \):
\[
S(8, 4) = \frac{40824}{24} = 1701
\]

Thus, the number of ways to split 8 individuals into 4 teams, each with at least 1 member, is \(\boxed{1701}\).<#sep#> moves and execute it.

### 1.1-1.5: Problem Analysis

**Problem Understanding:**
The issue is that Crowdin untranslated keys are being marked as empty strings ("") in the translation dictionaries. These empty strings are interpreted as valid translations by the system, causing empty strings to appear in the UI components instead of falling back to the default language.

**Key Technical Details:**
- File: `/testbed/sepal_ui/translator/translator.py` - The main translator module
- Method: `_update()` method (lines 198-218) - responsible for merging default and target dictionaries
- Test: `test_delete_empty` in `/testbed/tests/test_Translator.py` - suggests there should be a way to delete empty values

**Problem in Clear Terms:**
When Crowdin exports translations, untranslated keys get set to empty strings. The current translator implementation treats these empty strings as valid translations, preventing fallback to the default language. This results in empty UI elements instead of showing the original English text.

**Steps to Reproduce:**
1. Have a translation dictionary with some keys having empty string values (from Crowdin)
2. Initialize the Translator with this dictionary
3. Access those keys - they return empty strings instead of falling back to default language

**Best Practices for Fix:**
- Minimal changes to existing code
- Preserve existing functionality
- Ensure empty strings are treated as "missing translations" and fall back to default
- Don't break existing tests

import math
import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
import torch.nn as nn


class BertSelfAttention(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, config):
        super(BertSelfAttention, self).__init__()
        if config.hidden_size % config.num_attention_heads != 0:
            raise ValueError(
                'The hidden size (%d) is not a multiple of the number of attention heads (%d)'
                 % (config.hidden_size, config.num_attention_heads))
        self.num_attention_heads = config.num_attention_heads
        self.attention_head_size = int(config.hidden_size / config.
            num_attention_heads)
        self.all_head_size = (self.num_attention_heads * self.
            attention_head_size)
        self.query = nn.Linear(config.hidden_size, self.all_head_size)
        self.key = nn.Linear(config.hidden_size, self.all_head_size)
        self.value = nn.Linear(config.hidden_size, self.all_head_size)
        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(config.attention_probs_dropout_prob)

    def transpose_for_scores(self, x):
        new_x_shape = x.size()[:-1] + (self.num_attention_heads, self.
            attention_head_size)
        x = torch.reshape(x, new_x_shape)
        return x.permute(0, 2, 1, 3)

    def transpose_key_for_scores(self, x):
        new_x_shape = x.size()[:-1] + (self.num_attention_heads, self.
            attention_head_size)
        x = torch.reshape(x, new_x_shape)
        return x.permute(0, 2, 3, 1)

    def forward(self, hidden_states, attention_mask):
        mixed_query_layer = self.query(hidden_states)
        mixed_key_layer = self.key(hidden_states)
        mixed_value_layer = self.value(hidden_states)
        query_layer = self.transpose_for_scores(mixed_query_layer)
        key_layer = self.transpose_key_for_scores(mixed_key_layer)
        value_layer = self.transpose_for_scores(mixed_value_layer)
        attention_scores = torch.matmul(query_layer, key_layer)
        attention_scores = attention_scores / math.sqrt(self.
            attention_head_size)
        attention_scores = attention_scores + attention_mask
        attention_probs = F.softmax(attention_scores, dim=-1)
        attention_probs = self.dropout(attention_probs)
        context_layer = torch.matmul(attention_probs, value_layer)
        context_layer = context_layer.permute(0, 2, 1, 3).contiguous()
        new_context_layer_shape = context_layer.size()[:-2] + (self.
            all_head_size,)
        context_layer = torch.reshape(context_layer, new_context_layer_shape)
        return context_layer


def get_inputs():
    return [torch.rand([4, 4, 4]), torch.rand([4, 4, 4])]


def get_init_inputs():
    return [[], {'config': _mock_config(hidden_size=4, num_attention_heads=
        4, attention_probs_dropout_prob=0.5)}]

<#sep#>from _paritybench_helpers import _mock_config
from torch.nn import Module
import math
import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
import torch.nn as nn


class BertLayerNorm(Module):

    def __init__(self, hidden_size, eps=1e-12):
        super(BertLayerNorm, self).__init__()
        self.shape = torch.Size((hidden_size,))
        self.eps = eps
        self.weight = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(hidden_size))
        self.bias = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(hidden_size))

    def forward(self, x):
        u = x.mean(-1, keepdim=True)
        s = x - u
        s = s * s
        s = s.mean(-1, keepdim=True)
        x = (x - u) / torch.sqrt(s + self.eps)
        x = self.weight * x + self.bias
        return x


class BertSelfAttention(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, config):
        super(BertSelfAttention, self).__init__()
        if config.hidden_size % config.num_attention_heads != 0:
            raise ValueError(
                'The hidden size (%d) is not a multiple of the number of attention heads (%d)'
                 % (config.hidden_size, config.num_attention_heads))
        self.num_attention_heads = config.num_attention_heads
        self.attention_head_size = int(config.hidden_size / config.
            num_attention_heads)
        self.all_head_size = (self.num_attention_heads * self.
            attention_head_size)
        self.query = nn.Linear(config.hidden_size, self.all_head_size)
        self.key = nn.Linear(config.hidden_size, self.all_head_size)
        self.value = nn.Linear(config.hidden_size, self.all_head_size)
        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(config.attention_probs_dropout_prob)

    def transpose_for_scores(self, x):
        new_x_shape = x.size()[:-1] + (self.num_attention_heads, self.
            attention_head_size)
        x = torch.reshape(x, new_x_shape)
        return x.permute(0, 2, 1, 3)

    def transpose_key_for_scores(self, x):
        new_x_shape = x.size()[:-1] + (self.num_attention_heads, self.
            attention_head_size)
        x = torch.reshape(x, new_x_shape)
        return x.permute(0, 2, 3, 1)

    def forward(self, hidden_states, attention_mask):
        mixed_query_layer = self.query(hidden_states)
        mixed_key_layer = self.key(hidden_states)
        mixed_value_layer = self.value(hidden_states)
        query_layer = self.transpose_for_scores(mixed_query_layer)
        key_layer = self.transpose_key_for_scores(mixed_key_layer)
        value_layer = self.transpose_for_scores(mixed_value_layer)
        attention_scores = torch.matmul(query_layer, key_layer)
        attention_scores = attention_scores / math.sqrt(self.
            attention_head_size)
        attention_scores = attention_scores + attention_mask
        attention_probs = F.softmax(attention_scores, dim=-1)
        attention_probs = self.dropout(attention_probs)
        context_layer = torch.matmul(attention_probs, value_layer)
        context_layer = context_layer.permute(0, 2, 1, 3).contiguous()
        new_context_layer_shape = context_layer.size()[:-2] + (self.
            all_head_size,)
        context_layer = torch.reshape(context_layer, new_context_layer_shape)
        return context_layer


class BertSelfOutput(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, config):
        super(BertSelfOutput, self).__init__()
        self.dense = nn.Linear(config.hidden_size, config.hidden_size)
        self.LayerNorm = BertLayerNorm(config.hidden_size, eps=1e-12)
        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(config.hidden_dropout_prob)

    def forward(self, hidden_states, input_tensor):
        hidden_states = self.dense(hidden_states)
        hidden_states = self.dropout(hidden_states)
        hidden_states = self.LayerNorm(hidden_states + input_tensor)
        return hidden_states


class BertAttention(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, config):
        super(BertAttention, self).__init__()
        self.self = BertSelfAttention(config)
        self.output = BertSelfOutput(config)

    def forward(self, input_tensor, attention_mask):
        self_output = self.self(input_tensor, attention_mask)
        attention_output = self.output(self_output, input_tensor)
        return attention_output


def get_inputs():
    return [torch.rand([4, 4, 4]), torch.rand([4, 4, 4])]


def get_init_inputs():
    return [[], {'config': _mock_config(hidden_size=4, num_attention_heads=
        4, attention_probs_dropout_prob=0.5, hidden_dropout_prob=0.5)}]

Calculating coefficients:

44+144=188; 188+44=232. So:

16x^4 -96x^3 +232x² -264x +121.

Therefore, the inequality is:

4x² -16x +12 ≥ 16x^4 -96x^3 +232x² -264x +121.

Bring all terms to the left side:

4x² -16x +12 -16x^4 +96x^3 -232x² +264x -121 ≥0.

Combine like terms:

-16x^4 +96x^3 + (4x² -232x²) + (-16x +264x) + (12 -121) ≥0.

Calculates to:

-16x^4 +96x^3 -228x² +248x -109 ≥0.

Multiply both sides by -1 to make the leading coefficient positive, which will reverse the inequality sign:

16x^4 -96x^3 +228x² -248x +109 ≤0.

So now, we have to solve the inequality 16x^4 -96x^3 +228x² -248x +109 ≤0 for x ≥3.

This quartic inequality seems quite complex. Let's see if we can factor the quartic or find its roots. Maybe the quartic is a perfect square? Let's check.

Suppose it factors as (ax² + bx +c)^2. Let's compute:

(ax² +bx +c)^2 = a²x^4 + 2abx^3 + (2ac + b²)x² + 2bcx + c².

Comparing with 16x^4 -96x^3 +228x² -248x +109.

So,

a² =16 ⇒ a=4 or -4. Let's take a=4.

Then, 2ab = -96 ⇒ 2*4*b = -96 ⇒ 8b = -96 ⇒ b = -12.

Next, 2ac + b² =228. Let's plug in a=4 and b=-12.

2*4*c + (-12)^2 = 8c +144 =228 ⇒8c=84 ⇒c=10.5. Hmm, which is 21/2. But the constant term in the quartic is 109, but c² would be (21/2)^2 = 441/4 = 110.25. That's not matching 109. So it's not a perfect square.

Alternatively, maybe (4x² +bx +c)(4x² + dx +e). Let's try factoring.

Looking for factors such that:

(4x² +bx +c)(4x² +dx +e) =16x^4 + (4d +4b)x^3 + (bd +4e +4c)x² + (be +cd)x + ce.

We need:

16x^4 + (4d +4b)x^3 + (bd +4e +4c)x² + (be +cd)x + ce =16x^4 -96x^3 +228x² -248x +109.

Therefore, set up equations:

1) 4d +4b = -96 ⇒d +b = -24.

2) bd +4e +4c =228.

3) be + cd = -248.

4) ce =109.

Looking at equation 4: ce=109. 109 is a prime number, so possible integer solutions for c and e are (1,109), (109,1), (-1,-109), (-109,-1). Let's try c=1, e=109. Then, from equation 3: b*109 + d*1 =-248. But we also know from equation 1: d = -24 -b.

So substituting into equation 3: 109b + (-24 -b) = -248 ⇒108b -24 = -248 ⇒108b = -224 ⇒b= -224/108 = -56/27. Not integer, so maybe not helpful.

Try c=109, e=1. Then equation 3: b*1 + d*109 = -248. From equation 1: d= -24 -b. So substituting:

b + (-24 -b)*109 = -248 ⇒b -24*109 -109b = -248 ⇒(-108b) -2616 = -248 ⇒-108b = 2368 ⇒b= -2368/108 ≈ -21.9259. Not integer. Not helpful.

Now try c=-1, e=-109. Then equation 3: b*(-109) + d*(-1) = -248. From equation 1: d = -24 -b.

Plugging in: -109b - (-24 -b) = -248 ⇒-109b +24 +b = -248 ⇒-108b +24 = -248 ⇒-108b = -272 ⇒b=272/108=68/27. Not integer.

Similarly, c=-109, e=-1: equation 3: b*(-1) + d*(-109) = -248 ⇒ -b -109d = -248. With d = -24 -b, substitute:

-b -109*(-24 -b) = -248 ⇒-b +2616 +109b = -248 ⇒108b +2616 = -248 ⇒108b = -2864 ⇒b= -2864/108 = approx -26.5185. Not integer. So maybe this approach isn't working.

Alternatively, perhaps the quartic can be factored with a quadratic factor. Let's try using rational root theorem. The possible rational roots of the quartic equation 16x^4 -96x^3 +228x² -248x +109=0 are factors of 109 over factors of 16. Since 109 is prime, possible roots are ±1, ±109, ±1/2, etc. Let's test x=1: 16 -96 +228 -248 +109 = 16 -96= -80; -80+228=148; 148-248=-100; -100+109=9≠0. x=1 is not a root. x=109 is way too big. x=1/2: 16*(1/16) -96*(1/8) +228*(1/4) -248*(1/2)+109=1 -12 +57 -124 +109= (1-12)= -11; (-11+57)=46; (46-124)=-78; (-78+109)=31≠0. x= -1: 16 +96 +228 +248 +109= 16+96=112; 112+228=340; 340+248=588; 588+109=697≠0. So no rational roots here. Hmm.

sepal_ui/message/ar-SA/locale.json:<#sep#>import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F


def Linear(in_features, out_features, bias=True):
    m = nn.Linear(in_features, out_features, bias)
    nn.init.xavier_uniform_(m.weight)
    if bias:
        nn.init.constant_(m.bias, 0.0)
    return m


class TransformerDecoderLayer(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, d_model, nhead, dim_feedforward=2048, dropout=0.1,
        attention_dropout=0.1, activation_dropout=0.1, activation='relu',
        normalize_before=True):
        super(TransformerDecoderLayer, self).__init__()
        self.normalize_before = normalize_before
        self.self_attn = nn.MultiheadAttention(d_model, nhead, dropout=
            attention_dropout)
        self.multihead_attn = nn.MultiheadAttention(d_model, nhead, dropout
            =dropout)
        self.linear1 = Linear(d_model, dim_feedforward)
        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(dropout)
        self.linear2 = Linear(dim_feedforward, d_model)
        self.norm1 = nn.LayerNorm(d_model)
        self.norm2 = nn.LayerNorm(d_model)
        self.norm3 = nn.LayerNorm(d_model)
        self.activation_dropout = nn.Dropout(activation_dropout)
        self.activation = {'relu': F.relu, 'gelu': F.gelu}[activation]

    def forward(self, tgt, memory, tgt_mask=None, memory_mask=None,
        tgt_key_padding_mask=None, memory_key_padding_mask=None):
        """Pass the inputs (and mask) through the decoder layer.

        Args:
            tgt: the sequence to the decoder layer (required).
            memory: the sequnce from the last layer of the encoder (required).
            tgt_mask: the mask for the tgt sequence (optional).
            memory_mask: the mask for the memory sequence (optional).
            tgt_key_padding_mask: the mask for the tgt keys per batch (optional).
            memory_key_padding_mask: the mask for the memory keys per batch (optional).
        """
        residual = tgt
        if self.normalize_before:
            tgt = self.norm1(tgt)
        tgt = self.self_attn(tgt, tgt, tgt, attn_mask=tgt_mask,
            key_padding_mask=tgt_key_padding_mask)[0]
        tgt = residual + self.dropout(tgt)
        if not self.normalize_before:
            tgt = self.norm1(tgt)
        residual = tgt
        if self.normalize_before:
            tgt = self.norm2(tgt)
        tgt = self.multihead_attn(tgt, memory, memory, attn_mask=
            memory_mask, key_padding_mask=memory_key_padding_mask)[0]
        tgt = residual + self.dropout(tgt)
        if not self.normalize_before:
            tgt = self.norm2(tgt)
        residual = tgt
        if self.normalize_before:
            tgt = self.norm3(tgt)
        tgt = self.activation(self.linear1(tgt))
        tgt = self.activation_dropout(tgt)
        tgt = self.linear2(tgt)
        tgt = residual + self.dropout(tgt)
        if not self.normalize_before:
            tgt = self.norm3(tgt)
        return tgt


def get_inputs():
    return [torch.rand([4, 4]), torch.rand([4, 4])]


def get_init_inputs():
    return [[], {'d_model': 4, 'nhead': 4}]

sepal_ui/messag<#sep#>import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
from torch import nn


class AdaptiveInstanceNorm2d(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, num_features, eps=1e-05, momentum=0.1):
        super(AdaptiveInstanceNorm2d, self).__init__()
        self.num_features = num_features
        self.eps = eps
        self.momentum = momentum
        self.weight = None
        self.bias = None
        self.register_buffer('running_mean', torch.zeros(num_features))
        self.register_buffer('running_var', torch.ones(num_features))

    def forward(self, x):
        assert self.weight is not None and self.bias is not None, 'Please assign AdaIN weight first'
        b, c = x.size(0), x.size(1)
        running_mean = self.running_mean.repeat(b)
        running_var = self.running_var.repeat(b)
        x_reshaped = x.contiguous().view(1, b * c, *x.size()[2:])
        out = F.batch_norm(x_reshaped, running_mean, running_var, self.
            weight, self.bias, True, self.momentum, self.eps)
        return out.view(b, c, *x.size()[2:])

    def __repr__(self):
        return self.__class__.__name__ + '(' + str(self.num_features) + ')'


class Conv2dBlock(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, in_dim, out_dim, ks, st, padding=0, norm='none',
        activation='relu', pad_type='zero', use_bias=True, activation_first
        =False, dropout='none'):
        super(Conv2dBlock, self).__init__()
        self.use_bias = use_bias
        self.activation_first = activation_first
        if pad_type == 'reflect':
            self.pad = nn.ReflectionPad2d(padding)
        elif pad_type == 'replicate':
            self.pad = nn.ReplicationPad2d(padding)
        elif pad_type == 'zero':
            self.pad = nn.ZeroPad2d(padding)
        else:
            assert 0, 'Unsupported padding type: {}'.format(pad_type)
        norm_dim = out_dim
        if norm == 'bn':
            self.norm = nn.BatchNorm2d(norm_dim)
        elif norm == 'in':
            self.norm = nn.InstanceNorm2d(norm_dim)
        elif norm == 'adain':
            self.norm = AdaptiveInstanceNorm2d(norm_dim)
        elif norm == 'none':
            self.norm = None
        else:
            assert 0, 'Unsupported normalization: {}'.format(norm)
        if activation == 'relu':
            self.activation = nn.ReLU()
        elif activation == 'lrelu':
            self.activation = nn.LeakyReLU(0.2)
        elif activation == 'tanh':
            self.activation = nn.Tanh()
        elif activation == 'none':
            self.activation = None
        else:
            assert 0, 'Unsupported activation: {}'.format(activation)
        if dropout == 'none':
            self.dropout = None
        else:
            self.dropout = nn.Dropout(dropout)
        self.conv = nn.Conv2d(in_dim, out_dim, ks, st, bias=self.use_bias)

    def forward(self, x):
        if self.activation_first:
            if self.activation:
                x = self.activation(x)
            x = self.conv(self.pad(x))
            if self.norm:
                x = self.norm(x)
        else:
            x = self.conv(self.pad(x))
            if self.norm:
                x = self.norm(x)
            if self.activation:
                x = self.activation(x)
        if self.dropout:
            x = self.dropout(x)
        return x


class ActFirstResBlock(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, fin, fout, fhid=None, activation='lrelu', norm='none'):
        super().__init__()
        self.learned_shortcut = fin != fout
        self.fin = fin
        self.fout = fout
        self.fhid = min(fin, fout) if fhid is None else fhid
        self.conv_0 = Conv2dBlock(self.fin, self.fhid, 3, 1, padding=1,
            pad_type='reflect', norm=norm, activation=activation,
            activation_first=True)
        self.conv_1 = Conv2dBlock(self.fhid, self.fout, 3, 1, padding=1,
            pad_type='reflect', norm=norm, activation=activation,
            activation_first=True)
        if self.learned_shortcut:
            self.conv_s = Conv2dBlock(self.fin, self.fout, 1, 1, activation
                ='none', use_bias=False)

    def forward(self, x):
        x_s = self.conv_s(x) if self.learned_shortcut else x
        dx = self.conv_0(x)
        dx = self.conv_1(dx)
        out = x_s + dx
        return out


def get_inputs():
    return [torch.rand([4, 4, 4, 4])]


def get_init_inputs():
    return [[], {'fin': 4, 'fout': 4}]

</think>To solve the inequality \(\sqrt{x-1} + \sqrt{x-3} \geq \sqrt{(2x-3)^2 + 2x - 2}\), we start by simplifying the right-hand side. The expression under the square root on the right-hand side is:

\[
(2x-3)^2 + 2x - 2 = 4x^2 - 12x + 9 + 2x - 2 = 4x^2 - 10x + 7
\]

Thus, the inequality becomes:

\[
\sqrt{x-1} + \sqrt{x-3} \geq \sqrt{4x^2 - 10x + 7}
\]

Next, we determine the domain of the inequality. For the left-hand side, we need:

\[
x - 1 \geq 0 \quad \text{and} \quad x - 3 \geq 0 \implies x \geq 3
\]

For the right-hand side, since \(4x^2 - 10x + 7\) is always positive (as its discriminant is negative and the leading coefficient is positive), the domain is all real numbers. Therefore, the domain of the inequality is \(x \geq 3\).

We now square both sides of the inequality:

\[
(\sqrt{x-1} + \sqrt{x-3})^2 \geq 4x^2 - 10x + 7
\]

Expanding the left-hand side, we get:

\[
(x-1) + (x-3) + 2\sqrt{(x-1)(x-3)} \geq 4x^2 - 10x + 7
\]

Simplifying, we obtain:

\[
2x - 4 + 2\sqrt{(x-1)(x-3)} \geq 4x^2 - 10x + 7
\]

Rearranging terms, we get:

\[
2\sqrt{(x-1)(x-3)} \geq 4x^2 - 12x + 11
\]

Since both sides are non-negative for \(x \geq 3\), we can square both sides again:

\[
4(x-1)(x-3) \geq (4x^2 - 12x + 11)^2
\]

Expanding both sides, we get:

\[
4(x^2 - 4x + 3) \geq 16x^4 - 96x^3 + 232x^2 - 264x + 121
\]

Simplifying, we obtain:

\[
4x^2 - 16x + 12 \geq 16x^4 - 96x^3 + 232x^2 - 264x + 121
\]

Rearranging all terms to one side, we get:

\[
-16x^4 + 96x^3 - 228x^2 + 248x - 109 \geq 0
\]

Multiplying by \(-1\), we get:

\[
16x^4 - 96x^3 + 228x^2 - 248x + 109 \leq 0
\]

Wait wait:

Actually, to prefer closer to trolls, (distance) we want lower distances → higher 1/d, so higher score.

Also longer moves (higher move_distance_sq) give higher score.

Thus higher total score is desired.

Hence the priority is maximizing this score.

Thus, for each move, compute the score and choose the one with the maximum.

This combines both:

- getting closer to a troll (if can achieve that)

- moving further (such as in a hurl)

Hence:

Once all possible moves have scores computed, pick the one with the highest score.

This could be a viable heuristic.

Thus coding steps for non-kill part:

1. Collect all possible moves from all Dwarfs.

2. filter out unsafe moves (those moving to adjacent of troll's current pos).

3. Compute score for each filtered move based on the above.

4. choose the move with highest score.

If tie, pick arbitrarily (first in list due to sorting). 

Alternatively, sort descending by score and choose first.

Then proceed.

Thus, the move.to is the destination, and move.from is the starting position.

Thus, the code can proceed with this structure.

So putting it together, here's the step-by-step outline for the turn() function:

 turn() {

    // Check for any killing moves first.

    var killMoves = controller.killing_moves();

    if ( killMoves.length > 0 ) {

        // select first one, or best?

        let chosenMove = killMoves[0];

        // need to select the from space and move to the to space.

        // assume each move in killMoves has .to and .from ?

        // perhaps need to confirm but perhaps:

        controller.select_space( chosenMove.from.x, chosenMove.from.y );

        controller.move( chosenMove.to.x, chosenMove.to.y );

        return;

    }

    // else, find other moves.

    // Collect all possible moves.

    var possibleMoves = [];

    var dwarfs = controller.dwarfs();

    for ( var dwarf of dwarfs ) {

        let fromX = dwarf.x;

        let fromY = dwarf.y;

        let moveSpots = controller.check_space( fromX, fromY );

        for ( var spot of moveSpots ) {

            possibleMoves.push({

                from: {x: fromX, y: fromY},

                to: {x: spot.x, y: spot.y}

            });

        }

    }

    // filter out unsafe moves.

    var filteredMoves = possibleMoves.filter( move => {

        let toX = move.to.x;

        let toY = move.to.y;

        let safe = true;

        let trolls = controller.trolls();

        for ( let troll of trolls ) {

            let dx = toX - troll.x;

            let dy = toY - troll.y;

            if ( dx*dx + dy*dy < 2 ) {

                safe = false;

                break;

            }

        }

        return safe;

    });

    if ( filteredMoves.length === 0 ) {

        // No safe move? pick any possible move, even dangerous ones? Or pick first possible?

        // perhaps pick first possible move.

        if ( possibleMoves.length ==0 ) {

            // no moves available? Then the end?

            return; // but that's game over?

        }

        let anyMove = possibleMoves[0];

        controller.select_space( anyMove.from.x, anyMove.from.y );

        controller.move( anyMove.to.x, anyMove.to.y );

        return;

    }

    // Compute each filteredMove's score.

    for ( let move of filteredMoves ) {

        let dest = move.to;

        let minDist = Infinity;

        let trolls = controller.trolls();

        for ( let troll of trolls ) {

            let dx = dest.x - troll.x;

            let dy = dest.y - troll.y;

            let dist_sq = dx*dx + dy*dy;

            if ( dist_sq < minDist ) {

                minDist = dist_sq;

            }

        }

        let move_dx = dest.x - move.from.x;

        let move_dy = dest.y - move.from.y;

        let move_distance_sq = move_dx*move_dx + move_dy * move_dy;

        move.score = (1.0 / Math.sqrt(minDist)) + (move_distance_sq );

    }

    // Sort moves by score descending.

    filteredMoves.sort( (a,b) => (b.score - a.score) );

    let bestMove = filteredMoves[0];

    // make the move.

    controller.select_space( bestMove.from.x, bestMove.from.y );

    controller.move( bestMove.to.x, bestMove.to.y );

}

Wait, in the score's computation:

the first term was 1.0 / minDist_to_troll (distance squared? or actual distance?)

Wait in my previous explanation, I considered minDist was the squared distance. Since we are dealing with squared, then 1/sqrt( minDist ) is the actual inverse distance.

Thus:

distance to troll's squared is dx^2 + dy^2.

But the distance is sqrt(dx2 + dy2).

I want the inverse of this distance (so that closer trolls give higher score).

Thus, for each move's destination, find the troll at the smallest distance, then compute 1/distance.

But since that's sqrt(dx2+dy2):

the term is 1.0/Math.sqrt(minDist_sq).

The minDist_sq would be the squared distance to the closest troll.

Wait, minDist was set as the minimum over dx^2 + dy^2.

Thus the distance is sqrt(minDist_sq).

So the first term is 1/sqrt( minDist_sq ), which increases as the distance approaches zero (but the move is safe so distance must be at least sqrt(2)).

The second term is the squared move distance (how far the Dwarf moves), maximizing that.

Thus the score is prefer the closest troll proximity (max 1/sqrt(minDist) ) and the longest move distance.

Making the move with both the shortest distance to a troll (so max when close) and the furthest distance (long) the best.

This should move as long as possible towards any troll available.

If there's a choice between moving towards a closer or farther troll, closer wins in terms of distance term.

Thus, this seems a reasonable heuristic.

Possible edge cases:

- If there are no trolls left, but that's game over.

But the filtering would pass as no trolls to attack, so minDist is large, so all moves score similarly except for move distance.

Thus, Dwarfs would be moving as far as possible every time.

That could be good to keep spreading out? Or only towards remaining enemies, but there are none, so just move outward.

Not sure but okay.

Phase<#sep#>import math
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F


def attention(q, k, v, d_k, mask=None, dropout=None):
    scores = torch.matmul(q, k.transpose(-2, -1)) / math.sqrt(d_k)
    if mask is not None:
        mask = mask.unsqueeze(1)
        scores = scores.masked_fill(mask == 0, -1000000000.0)
    scores = F.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
    if dropout is not None:
        scores = dropout(scores)
    output = torch.matmul(scores, v)
    return output


class FeedForward(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, d_model, d_ff=2048, dropout=0.1):
        super().__init__()
        self.linear_1 = nn.Linear(d_model, d_ff)
        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(dropout)
        self.linear_2 = nn.Linear(d_ff, d_model)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = self.dropout(F.relu(self.linear_1(x)))
        x = self.linear_2(x)
        return x


class MultiHeadAttention(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, heads, d_model, dropout=0.1):
        super().__init__()
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.d_k = d_model // heads
        self.h = heads
        self.q_linear = nn.Linear(d_model, d_model)
        self.v_linear = nn.Linear(d_model, d_model)
        self.k_linear = nn.Linear(d_model, d_model)
        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(dropout)
        self.out = nn.Linear(d_model, d_model)

    def forward(self, q, k, v, mask=None):
        bs = q.size(0)
        k = self.k_linear(k).view(bs, -1, self.h, self.d_k)
        q = self.q_linear(q).view(bs, -1, self.h, self.d_k)
        v = self.v_linear(v).view(bs, -1, self.h, self.d_k)
        k = k.transpose(1, 2)
        q = q.transpose(1, 2)
        v = v.transpose(1, 2)
        scores = attention(q, k, v, self.d_k, mask, self.dropout)
        concat = scores.transpose(1, 2).contiguous().view(bs, -1, self.d_model)
        output = self.out(concat)
        return output


class Norm(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, d_model, eps=1e-06):
        super().__init__()
        self.size = d_model
        self.alpha = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(self.size))
        self.bias = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(self.size))
        self.eps = eps

    def forward(self, x):
        norm = self.alpha * (x - x.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)) / (x.std(dim
            =-1, keepdim=True) + self.eps) + self.bias
        return norm


class DecoderLayer(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, d_model, heads, dropout=0.1):
        super().__init__()
        self.norm_1 = Norm(d_model)
        self.norm_2 = Norm(d_model)
        self.norm_3 = Norm(d_model)
        self.dropout_1 = nn.Dropout(dropout)
        self.dropout_2 = nn.Dropout(dropout)
        self.dropout_3 = nn.Dropout(dropout)
        self.attn_1 = MultiHeadAttention(heads, d_model, dropout=dropout)
        self.attn_2 = MultiHeadAttention(heads, d_model, dropout=dropout)
        self.ff = FeedForward(d_model, dropout=dropout)

    def forward(self, x, e_outputs, src_mask, trg_mask):
        x2 = self.norm_1(x)
        x = x + self.dropout_1(self.attn_1(x2, x2, x2, trg_mask))
        x2 = self.norm_2(x)
        x = x + self.dropout_2(self.attn_2(x2, e_outputs, e_outputs, src_mask))
        x2 = self.norm_3(x)
        x = x + self.dropout_3(self.ff(x2))
        return x


def get_inputs():
    return [torch.rand([4, 4, 4]), torch.rand([4, 4, 4]), torch.rand([4, 4,
        4]), torch.rand([4, 4, 4])]


def get_init_inputs():
    return [[], {'d_model': 4, 'heads': 4}]
<#sep#> one side, we get:

\[
-16x^4 + 96x^3 - 228x^2 + 248x - 109 \geq 0
\]

Multiplying by \(-1\), we get:

\[
16x^4 - 96x^3 + 228x^2 - 248x + 109 \leq 0
\]

We now analyze the quartic polynomial \(16x^4 - 96x^3 + 228x^2 - 248x + 109\). Evaluating at \(x = 3\):

\[
16(3)^4 - 96(3)^3 + 228(3)^2 - 248(3) + 109 = 1296 - 2592 + 2052 - 744 + 109 = 121 > 0
\]

Since the quartic polynomial is positive at \(x = 3\) and its leading coefficient is positive, it tends to \(\infty\) as \(x \to \infty\). Therefore, the polynomial \(16x^4 - 96x^3 + 228x^2 - 248x + 109\) is always positive for \(x \geq 3\). Thus, the inequality \(16x^4 - 96x^3 + 228x^2 - 248x + 109 \leq 0\) has no solutions.

Therefore, the original inequality \(\sqrt{x-1} + \sqrt{x-3} \geq \sqrt{4x^2 - 10x + 7}\) has no solutions in the domain \(x \geq 3\).

The final answer is:

\[
\boxed{\emptyset}
\]<#sep#>.

But in the code, the heuristic should choose the Dwarfs that can move further towards.

Moreover, in cases where there are multiple options, like form lines to enable hurling, but the current heuristic doesn’t account for that.

But this would take this into account to a degree? Long movement (hurls would have large move_distance_sq values) would be preferred.

So the heuristic may choose hurl moves first, as they can have larger move distance (e.g., moving 3 spaces by hurl gives a larger square term).

Thus, the code may encourage the optimal hurl moves.

But for example, if there are three Dwarfs in a line (size 3):

Then a hurl could move the end Dwarfs 3 spaces, giving large move_distance_sq (3^2 =9).

Which would add to the score, which is compared to moving via walk. 

Thus, that move would be picked above shorter moves.

Thus this logic should favor using hurl moves in such occasions.

Hence, the code should form a fairly reasonable strategy.

Now, implementing end_turn() function:

The end_turn() function is called after the player's turn completes but before the next turn starts.

Here, the player can declare if they think the game is over. Often, the game end is when one side has no pieces.

But the problem says one can declare the game over during their turn. So in end_turn() the decision must note whether to declare the game over according to the Dwarf.

The rules state that the game ends when either player declares it per turn AND the other player also declares it, but also when either team has no pieces or after 500 turns.

The Dwarf's client should only declare it over when it believes there are no moves or it's in a position where game should end.

A basic approach is:

check if there are no Dwarfs left or no trolls, in which case the game is over.

Also, perhaps declare the game over when there are no more moves for Dwarfs. But that might be handled automatically via the turn() function.

Thus in end_turn() :

 this (client's) decision whether to declare game over. The criteria could be:

- if there are no Dwarfs or no Trolls → declare yes.

 var myPieces = controller.pieces(); // returns all Dwarfs, since it's a Dwarf's end_turn()

 if ( myPieces.length ==0 || controller.trolls().length ==0 ) {

    controller.declare(true);

 else {

     controller.declare(false);

 }

Wait the declare function is a way to state if the player thinks the game should end.

Thus, in code:

function end_turn(){

    // Check if game is over.

    if ( controller.dwarfs().length ==0 || controller.trolls().length ==0 ) {

        // can declare to signal.

        controller.declare(true);

    } else {

        controller.declare(false);

    }

    // also, check if opp has declared.

    // But possibly no need, just our own decision.

}

Alternatively, perhaps even if there are pieces, but no moves possible, then it might be in a stalemate. But that's hard to compute.

Thus this gives the minimal condition checking.

Now assembling all together into a class.

The client's class (for Dwarf) must have:

Thus, writing this in code:

class DwarfKiller {
    constructor(controller) {
        this.controller = controller;
    }

    turn() {
        const killingMoves = this.controller.killing_moves();
        if (killingMoves.length > 0) {
            // Select the first killing move.
            const move = killingMoves[0];
            this.controller.select_space(move.from.x, move.from.y);
            this.controller.move(move.to.x, move.to.y);
            return;
        }

        let possibleMoves = [];
        
        const dwarfs = this.controller.dwarfs();
        for (const dwarf of dwarfs) {
            const fromX = dwarf.x;
            const fromY = dwarf.y;
            const moveOptions = this.controller.check_space(fromX, fromY);
            for (const spot of moveOptions) {
                possibleMoves.push({
                    from: {x: fromX, y: fromY},
                    to: {x: spot.x, y: spot.y}
                });
            }
        }

        const filteredMoves = possibleMoves.filter(move => {
            const {x: toX, y: toY} = move.to;
            const trolls = this.controller.trolls();
            for (const troll of trolls) {
                const dx = toX - troll.x;
                const dy = toY - troll.y;
                if (dx * dx + dy * dy < 2) {
                    return false;
                }
            }
            return true;
        });

        if (filteredMoves.length === 0) {
            if (possibleMoves.length > 0) {
                // Take the first possible move despite the danger
                const randomMove = possibleMoves[0];
                this.controller.select_space(randomMove.from.x, randomMove.from.y);
                this.controller.move(randomMove.to.x, randomMove.to.y);
            }
            return;
        }

        // Compute scores for filtered moves
        const computeScore = (move) => {
            const {x: toX, y: toY} = move.to;
            let minDistSq = Infinity;
            const trolls = this.controller.trolls();
            for (const troll of trolls) {
                const dx = toX - troll.x;
                const dy = toY - troll.y;
                const dist_sq = dx * dx + dy * dy;
                if (dist_sq < minDistSq) {
                    minDistSq = dist_sq;
                }
            }
            const move_dx = toX - move.from.x;
            const move_dy = toY - move.from.y;
            const moveDistanceSq = move_dx * move_dx + move_dy * move_dy;
            const invDist = minDistSq > 0 ? 1 / Math.sqrt(minDistSq) : 0; // Avoid division by zero
            return invDist + moveDistanceSq;
        };

        filteredMoves.forEach(move => move.score = computeScore(move));

        filteredMoves.sort((a, b) => b.score - a.score);

        const bestMove = filteredMoves[0];
        this.controller.select_space(bestMove.from.x, bestMove.from.y);
        this.controller.move(bestMove.to.x, bestMove.to.y);
    }

    end_turn() {
        const dwarfs = this.controller.dwarfs();
        const trolls = this.controller.trolls();
        if (dwarfs.length === 0 || trolls.length === 0) {
            this.controller.declare(true);
        } else {
            this.controller.declare(false);
        }
    }
}

Now, testing this code:

Potential edge cases:

- When a possible Move is aligned for a line but the code doesn't detect. But the priority for long moves should capture that.

- Handling safety incorrectly: if a move is filtered correctly?

Testing in `filteredMoves`, moves that are adjacent to a Troll are excluded.

- What if a troll can move into the Dwarf's position diagonally?

 The safety filter uses distance squared <2? For example, a position diagonally adjacent (dx=1, dy=1), distance squared is 2 → so if any troll is there, the move's destination would have a troll at distance squared < 2?:

 No, the condition is if (dx^2 + dy^2) <2 → for distance squared 2, it would be 2<2 → false.

 Thus a destination diagonally adjacent to any troll (squared distance 2) is considered safe.

 Thus, if a troll is two steps away but in that position, the move is allowed.

 But next turn, that troll could move diagonally in one step to a position now adjacent to the DWar?

 Wait no, the trolls can only move one space per turn (walk), so the position diagonally distant allows the troll to move into the row or column and come close.

 But next turn, the Dwarf may be moved elsewhere.

Hard to say, but the AI's heuristic may address this with other moves.

 Overall, this code is a heuristic-based approach that:

1. Prioritizes kills.

2. Moves towards threats safely (i.e., moving into positions that are as close as possible to trolls but without being immediately vulnerable.

3. Using longer moves to optimize for future opportunities and .

This should provide a reasonable base AI to start with.

   <#sep#>import math
import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
from torch.nn import Parameter
import torch.utils.data


def uniform(size, tensor):
    bound = 1.0 / math.sqrt(size)
    if tensor is not None:
        tensor.data.uniform_(-bound, bound)


class DenseSAGEConv(torch.nn.Module):
    """See :class:`torch_geometric.nn.conv.SAGEConv`.
    """

    def __init__(self, in_channels, out_channels, normalize=False, bias=True):
        super(DenseSAGEConv, self).__init__()
        self.in_channels = in_channels
        self.out_channels = out_channels
        self.normalize = normalize
        self.weight = Parameter(torch.Tensor(self.in_channels, out_channels))
        if bias:
            self.bias = Parameter(torch.Tensor(out_channels))
        else:
            self.register_parameter('bias', None)
        self.reset_parameters()

    def reset_parameters(self):
        uniform(self.in_channels, self.weight)
        uniform(self.in_channels, self.bias)

    def forward(self, x, adj, mask=None, add_loop=True):
        """
        Args:
            x (Tensor): Node feature tensor :math:`\\mathbf{X} \\in \\mathbb{R}^{B
                \\times N \\times F}`, with batch-size :math:`B`, (maximum)
                number of nodes :math:`N` for each graph, and feature
                dimension :math:`F`.
            adj (Tensor): Adjacency tensor :math:`\\mathbf{A} \\in \\mathbb{R}^{B
                \\times N \\times N}`. The adjacency tensor is broadcastable in
                the batch dimension, resulting in a shared adjacency matrix for
                the complete batch.
            mask (BoolTensor, optional): Mask matrix
                :math:`\\mathbf{M} \\in {\\{ 0, 1 \\}}^{B \\times N}` indicating
                the valid nodes for each graph. (default: :obj:`None`)
            add_loop (bool, optional): If set to :obj:`False`, the layer will
                not automatically add self-loops to the adjacency matrices.
                (default: :obj:`True`)
        """
        x = x.unsqueeze(0) if x.dim() == 2 else x
        adj = adj.unsqueeze(0) if adj.dim() == 2 else adj
        B, N, _ = adj.size()
        if add_loop:
            adj = adj.clone()
            idx = torch.arange(N, dtype=torch.long, device=adj.device)
            adj[:, idx, idx] = 1
        out = torch.matmul(adj, x)
        out = out / adj.sum(dim=-1, keepdim=True).clamp(min=1)
        out = torch.matmul(out, self.weight)
        if self.bias is not None:
            out = out + self.bias
        if self.normalize:
            out = F.normalize(out, p=2, dim=-1)
        if mask is not None:
            out = out * mask.view(B, N, 1)
        return out

    def __repr__(self):
        return '{}({}, {})'.format(self.__class__.__name__, self.
            in_channels, self.out_channels)


def get_inputs():
    return [torch.rand([4, 4, 4, 4]), torch.rand([4, 4, 4])]


def get_init_inputs():
    return [[], {'in_channels': 4, 'out_channels': 4}]

 Overall, this code is a heuristic-based approach that:

1. Prioritizes kills.

3. Using longer moves to optimize for future opportunities and .

# SmartDwarf  
SmartDwarf

```javascript
class SmartDwarf {
    constructor(controller) {
        this.controller = controller;
    }

    // Evaluate the best move based on closeness to trolls and safety
    turn() {
        const controller = this.controller;

        // Check for killing moves first
        const killingMoves = controller.killing_moves();
        if (killingMoves.length > 0) {
            const move = killingMoves[0]; // Prioritize first killing move
            controller.select_space(move.from.x, move.from.y);
            controller.move(move.to.x, move.to.y);
            return;
        }

        // Gather all possible moves
        const possibleMoves = [];
        const dwarfs = controller.dwarfs();
        for (const dwarf of dwarfs) {
            const fromX = dwarf.x;
            const fromY = dwarf.y;
            const destinations = controller.check_space(fromX, fromY);
            for (const dest of destinations) {
                possibleMoves.push({
                    from: { x: fromX, y: fromY },
                    to: { x: dest.x, y: dest.y }
                });
            }
        }

        // Filter out unsafe moves (adjacent to trolls)
        const filteredMoves = possibleMoves.filter(move => {
            const { x: toX, y: toY } = move.to;
            let safe = true;
            const trolls = controller.trolls();
            for (const troll of trolls) {
                const dx = toX - troll.x;
                const dy = toY - troll.y;
                if (dx * dx + dy * dy < 2) { // Adjacent or diagonally adjacent
                    safe = false;
                    break;
                }
            }
            return safe;
        });

        if (filteredMoves.length === 0) {
            // Fallback: pick any move if no safe options
            if (possibleMoves.length > 0) {
                const move = possibleMoves[0];
                controller.select_space(move.from.x, move.from.y);
                controller.move(move.to.x, move.to.y);
            }
            return;
        }

        // Compute score for each move
        const computeScore = (move) => {
            const { x: toX, y: toY } = move.to;
            let minDist = Infinity;
            const trolls = controller.trolls();
            for (const troll of trolls) {
                const dx = toX - troll.x;
                const dy = toY - troll.y;
                const dist_sq = dx * dx + dy * dy;
                if (dist_sq < minDist) minDist = dist_sq;
            }
            // Prefer closer to trolls and longer moves
            const distScore = minDist ? 1 / Math.sqrt(minDist) : 0;
            const moveDistance = (move.to.x - move.from.x) ** 2 + (move.to.y - move.from.y) ** 2;
            return distScore + moveDistance;
        };

        filteredMoves.forEach(move => move.score = computeScore(move));
        filteredMoves.sort((a, b) => b.score - a.score);

        const bestMove = filteredMoves[0];
        controller.select_space(bestMove.from.x, bestMove.from.y);
        controller.move(bestMove.to.x, bestMove.to.y);
    }

    // Declare game over when there are no pieces left for either side
    end_turn() {
        const dwarfs = this.controller.dwarfs();
        const trolls = this.controller.trolls();
        if (dwarfs.length === 0 || trolls.length === 0) {
            this.controller.declare(true);
        } else {
            this.controller.declare(false);
        }
    }
}
```

### Explanation:
- **Killing Priority**: The AI first checks for moves that can kill trolls using `killing_moves()`, prioritizing these to maximize score swings.
- **Safety Filter**: Moves are filtered to avoid positions where a dwarf could be killed by trolls in the next move, by checking adjacency to existing trolls.
- **Strategic Moves**: After eliminating dangerous options, the AI evaluates remaining moves based on:
  - **Closeness to Trolls**: Shorter distance to nearest troll improves score.
  - **Move Distance**: Longer moves are prioritized (e.g., hurl moves by forming lines).
- **Fallback**: If no safe moves exist, it defaults to any available move to avoid stalling.
- **Game Over Declaration**: Ends the game when either side has no pieces left.

This strategy balances aggressive troll elimination with safer positioning to ensure dwarves survive long enough to strategize further.<#sep#>.com/v1/badges/861f09002bb9d75b6ea5/maintainability

   101	            "module_l10n = sepal_ui.bin.module<#sep#>import math
import torch
from torch import nn
from torch.nn.parameter import Parameter


def linearExcitability(input, weight, excitability=None, bias=None):
    """Applies a linear transformation to the incoming data: :math:`y = c(xA^T) + b`.

    Shape:
        - input:        :math:`(N, *, in_features)`
        - weight:       :math:`(out_features, in_features)`
        - excitability: :math:`(out_features)`
        - bias:         :math:`(out_features)`
        - output:       :math:`(N, *, out_features)`
    (NOTE: `*` means any number of additional dimensions)"""
    if excitability is not None:
        output = torch.matmul(input, weight.t()) * excitability
    else:
        output = torch.matmul(input, weight.t())
    if bias is not None:
        output = output + bias
    return output


class LinearExcitability(nn.Module):
    """Module for a linear transformation with multiplicative excitability-parameter (i.e., learnable) and/or -buffer.

    Args:
        in_features:    size of each input sample
        out_features:   size of each output sample
        bias:           if 'False', layer will not learn an additive bias-parameter (DEFAULT=True)
        excitability:   if 'True', layer will learn a multiplicative excitability-parameter (DEFAULT=False)
        excit_buffer:   if 'True', layer will have excitability-buffer whose value can be set (DEFAULT=False)

    Shape:
        - input:    :math:`(N, *, in_features)` where `*` means any number of additional dimensions
        - output:   :math:`(N, *, out_features)` where all but the last dimension are the same shape as the input.

    Attributes:
        weight:         the learnable weights of the module of shape (out_features x in_features)
        excitability:   the learnable multiplication terms (out_features)
        bias:           the learnable bias of the module of shape (out_features)
        excit_buffer:   fixed multiplication variable (out_features)"""

    def __init__(self, in_features, out_features, bias=True, excitability=
        False, excit_buffer=False):
        super(LinearExcitability, self).__init__()
        self.in_features = in_features
        self.out_features = out_features
        self.weight = Parameter(torch.Tensor(out_features, in_features))
        if excitability:
            self.excitability = Parameter(torch.Tensor(out_features))
        else:
            self.register_parameter('excitability', None)
        if bias:
            self.bias = Parameter(torch.Tensor(out_features))
        else:
            self.register_parameter('bias', None)
        if excit_buffer:
            buffer = torch.ones(out_features)
            self.register_buffer('excit_buffer', buffer)
        else:
            self.register_buffer('excit_buffer', None)
        self.reset_parameters()

    def reset_parameters(self):
        """Modifies the parameters to initialize / reset them at appropriate values."""
        """Avoid using in-place init methods"""
        stdv = 1.0 / math.sqrt(self.weight.size(1))
        self.weight = Parameter(torch.distributions.Uniform(-stdv, stdv).
            sample(self.weight.size()))
        if self.excitability is not None:
            self.excitability = Parameter(torch.ones_like(self.excitability))
        if self.bias is not None:
            self.bias = Parameter(torch.distributions.Uniform(-stdv, stdv).
                sample(self.bias.size()))

    def forward(self, input):
        """Running this model's forward step requires/returns:
            -[input]:   [batch_size]x[...]x[in_features]
            -[output]:  [batch_size]x[...]x[hidden_features]"""
        if self.excit_buffer is None:
            excitability = self.excitability
        elif self.excitability is None:
            excitability = self.excit_buffer
        else:
            excitability = self.excitability * self.excit_buffer
        return linearExcitability(input, self.weight, excitability, self.bias)

    def __repr__(self):
        return self.__class__.__name__ + '(' + 'in_features=' + str(self.
            in_features) + ', out_features=' + str(self.out_features) + ')'

    return [torch.rand([4, 4, 4, 4])]

    return [[], {'in_features': 4, 'out_features': 4}]

Each point has one of 3 colors: red, green, or blue.

Roy and Biv would like to connect all the points with some edges. Edges can be drawn between any of the two of the given points. The cost of an edge is equal to the distance between the two points it connects.

However, there is a catch: Roy cannot see the color red and Biv cannot see the color blue.

Therefore, they have to choose the edges in such a way that if all the red points are removed, the remaining blue and green points are connected (and similarly, if all the blue points are removed, the remaining red and green points are connected).

Help them compute the minimum cost way to choose edges to satisfy the above constraints.
Input

The first line will contain an integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 300 000), the number of points.

The next n lines will contain two tokens pi and ci (pi is an integer, 1 ≤ pi ≤ 109, ci is a uppercase English letter 'R', 'G' or 'B'), denoting the position of the i-th point and the color of the i-th point. 'R' means red, 'G' denotes green, and 'B' means blue. The positions will be in strictly increasing order.

Output

Print a single integer, the minimum cost way to solve the problem.

Examples

Input

4
1 G
5 R
10 B
15 G


Output

23


Input

4
1 G
2 R
3 B
10 G


Output

12

Note

In the first sample, it is optimal to draw edges between the points (1,2), (1,4), (3,4). These have costs 4, 14, 5, respectively.

    <#sep#>import math
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F


def attention(q, k, v, d_k, mask=None, dropout=None):
    scores = torch.matmul(q, k.transpose(-2, -1)) / math.sqrt(d_k)
    if mask is not None:
        mask = mask.unsqueeze(1)
        scores = scores.masked_fill(mask == 0, -1000000000.0)
    scores = F.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
    if dropout is not None:
        scores = dropout(scores)
    output = torch.matmul(scores, v)
    return output


class FeedForward(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, d_model, d_ff=2048, dropout=0.1):
        super().__init__()
        self.linear_1 = nn.Linear(d_model, d_ff)
        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(dropout)
        self.linear_2 = nn.Linear(d_ff, d_model)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = self.dropout(F.relu(self.linear_1(x)))
        x = self.linear_2(x)
        return x


class MultiHeadAttention(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, heads, d_model, dropout=0.1):
        super().__init__()
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.d_k = d_model // heads
        self.h = heads
        self.q_linear = nn.Linear(d_model, d_model)
        self.v_linear = nn.Linear(d_model, d_model)
        self.k_linear = nn.Linear(d_model, d_model)
        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(dropout)
        self.out = nn.Linear(d_model, d_model)

    def forward(self, q, k, v, mask=None):
        bs = q.size(0)
        k = self.k_linear(k).view(bs, -1, self.h, self.d_k)
        q = self.q_linear(q).view(bs, -1, self.h, self.d_k)
        v = self.v_linear(v).view(bs, -1, self.h, self.d_k)
        k = k.transpose(1, 2)
        q = q.transpose(1, 2)
        v = v.transpose(1, 2)
        scores = attention(q, k, v, self.d_k, mask, self.dropout)
        concat = scores.transpose(1, 2).contiguous().view(bs, -1, self.d_model)
        output = self.out(concat)
        return output


class Norm(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, d_model, eps=1e-06):
        super().__init__()
        self.size = d_model
        self.alpha = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(self.size))
        self.bias = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(self.size))
        self.eps = eps

    def forward(self, x):
        norm = self.alpha * (x - x.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)) / (x.std(dim
            =-1, keepdim=True) + self.eps) + self.bias
        return norm


class EncoderLayer(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, d_model, heads, dropout=0.1):
        super().__init__()
        self.norm_1 = Norm(d_model)
        self.norm_2 = Norm(d_model)
        self.attn = MultiHeadAttention(heads, d_model, dropout=dropout)
        self.ff = FeedForward(d_model, dropout=dropout)
        self.dropout_1 = nn.Dropout(dropout)
        self.dropout_2 = nn.Dropout(dropout)

    def forward(self, x, mask):
        x2 = self.norm_1(x)
        x = x + self.dropout_1(self.attn(x2, x2, x2, mask))
        x2 = self.norm_2(x)
        x = x + self.dropout_2(self.ff(x2))
        return x


def get_inputs():
    return [torch.rand([4, 4, 4]), torch.rand([4, 4, 4])]


def get_init_inputs():
    return [[], {'d_model': 4, 'heads': 4}]

    13	class Translator(SimpleNamespace):
    14	    """
    15	    The translator is a SimpleNamespace of Simplenamespace. It reads 2 Json files, the first one being the source language (usually English) and the second one the target language.
    16	    It will replace in the source dictionary every key that exist in both json dictionaries. Following this procedure, every message that is not translated can still be accessed in the source language.
    17	    To access the dictionary keys, instead of using [], you can simply use key name as in an object ex: translator.first_key.secondary_key.
    18	    There are no depth limits, just respect the snake_case convention when naming your keys in the .json files.
    19	
    20	    Args:
    21	        json_folder (str | pathlib.Path): The folder where the dictionaries are stored
    22	        target (str, optional): The language code (IETF BCP 47) of the target lang (it should be the same as the target dictionary). Default to either the language specified in the parameter file or the default one.
    23	        default (str, optional): The language code (IETF BCP 47) of the source lang. default to "en" (it should be the same as the source dictionary)
    24	    """
    25	
    26	    FORBIDDEN_KEYS = [
    27	        "default_dict",
    28	        "target_dict",
    29	        "in",

    94	            setattr(self, k, getattr(ms, k))

    96	    @versionadded(version="2.7.0")

    98	    def find_target(folder, target=None):
    99	        """
   100	        find the target language in the available language folder
   101	
   102	        given a folder and a target lang, this function returns the closest language available in the folder
   103	        If nothing is found falling back to any working subvariety and return None if it doesn't exist
   104	
   105	        Args:
   106	            folder (pathlib.Path): the folder where the languages dictionnaries are stored
   107	            target (str, optional): the target lang in IETF BCP 47. If not specified, the value in the sepal-ui config file will be used
   108	
   109	        Return:
   110	            (bool, str): a bool to tell if the exact requested lan were available and the closest lang in IETF BCP 47
   111	        """
   112	
   113	        # init lang
   114	        lang = None
   115	
   116	        # if target is not set try to find one in the config file
   117	        # exit with none if the config file is not yet existing
   118	        if target is None:
   119	            if config_file.is_file():
   120	                config = ConfigParser()
   121	                config.read(config_file)
   122	                target = config.get("sepal-ui", "locale", fallback="en")
   123	            else:
   124	                return ("en", None)
   125	
   126	        # first scenario the target is available
   127	        if (folder / target).is_dir():
   128	            lang = target
   129	        # second senario the "main lang" is set
   130	        elif (folder / target[:2]).is_dir():
   131	            lang = target[:2]
   132	        # third scenario we search for any closely related language
   133	        else:
   134	            try:
   135	                f = next(f for f in folder.glob(f"{target[:2]}-*") if f.is_dir())
   136	                lang = f.stem
   137	            except StopIteration:
   138	                pass
   139	
   140	        return (target, lang)

   146	
   147	        Args:

   149	            key (str): the key to look for
   150	        """
   151	
   152	        for k, v in d.items():

   154	                cls.search_key(v, key)

   157	                    raise Exception(

import torch
from torch.nn import Parameter
import torch.utils.data


def uniform(size, tensor):
    bound = 1.0 / math.sqrt(size)
    if tensor is not None:
        tensor.data.uniform_(-bound, bound)


class DenseGraphConv(torch.nn.Module):
    """See :class:`torch_geometric.nn.conv.GraphConv`.
    """

    def __init__(self, in_channels, out_channels, aggr='add', bias=True):
        assert aggr in ['add', 'mean', 'max']
        super(DenseGraphConv, self).__init__()
        self.in_channels = in_channels
        self.out_channels = out_channels
        self.aggr = aggr
        self.weight = Parameter(torch.Tensor(in_channels, out_channels))
        self.lin = torch.nn.Linear(in_channels, out_channels, bias=bias)
        self.reset_parameters()

    def reset_parameters(self):
        uniform(self.in_channels, self.weight)
        self.lin.reset_parameters()

    def forward(self, x, adj, mask=None):
        """
        Args:
            x (Tensor): Node feature tensor :math:`\\mathbf{X} \\in \\mathbb{R}^{B
                \\times N \\times F}`, with batch-size :math:`B`, (maximum)
                number of nodes :math:`N` for each graph, and feature
                dimension :math:`F`.
            adj (Tensor): Adjacency tensor :math:`\\mathbf{A} \\in \\mathbb{R}^{B
                \\times N \\times N}`. The adjacency tensor is broadcastable in
                the batch dimension, resulting in a shared adjacency matrix for
                the complete batch.
            mask (BoolTensor, optional): Mask matrix
                :math:`\\mathbf{M} \\in {\\{ 0, 1 \\}}^{B \\times N}` indicating
                the valid nodes for each graph. (default: :obj:`None`)
        """
        x = x.unsqueeze(0) if x.dim() == 2 else x
        adj = adj.unsqueeze(0) if adj.dim() == 2 else adj
        B, N, _ = adj.size()
        out = torch.matmul(adj, x)
        out = torch.matmul(out, self.weight)
        if self.aggr == 'mean':
            out = out / adj.sum(dim=-1, keepdim=True).clamp(min=1)
        elif self.aggr == 'max':
            out = out.max(dim=-1)[0]
        out = out + self.lin(x)
        if mask is not None:
            out = out * mask.view(B, N, 1)
        return out

    def __repr__(self):
        return '{}({}, {})'.format(self.__class__.__name__, self.
            in_channels, self.out_channels)


def get_inputs():
    return [torch.rand([4, 4, 4, 4]), torch.rand([4, 4, 4])]


def get_init_inputs():
    return [[], {'in_channels': 4, 'out_channels': 4}]

</think>To evaluate the integral \(\int \frac{dx}{(e^x + e^{-x})^3}\), we start by simplifying the denominator. Notice that \(e^x + e^{-x} = 2 \cosh(x)\). Thus, the integral becomes:

\[
\int \frac{dx}{(2 \cosh(x))^3} = \frac{1}{8} \int \text{sech}^3(x) \, dx
\]

Next, we need to evaluate \(\int \text{sech}^3(x) \, dx\). We use integration by parts. Let:

\[
u = \text{sech}(x) \quad \text{and} \quad dv = \text{sech}^2(x) \, dx
\]

Then:

\[
du = -\text{sech}(x) \tanh(x) \, dx \quad \text{and} \quad v = \tanh(x)
\]

Using the integration by parts formula \(\int u \, dv = uv - \int v \, du\), we get:

\[
\int \text{sech}^3(x) \, dx = \text{sech}(x) \tanh(x) - \int \tanh(x) (-\text{sech}(x) \tanh(x)) \, dx
\]

Simplifying the integral on the right-hand side:

\[
\int \text{sech}^3(x) \, dx = \text{sech}(x) \tanh(x) + \int \text{sech}(x) \tanh^2(x) \, dx
\]

Using the identity \(\tanh^2(x) = 1 - \text{sech}^2(x)\), we have:

\[
\int \text{sech}^3(x) \, dx = \text{sech}(x) \tanh(x) + \int \text{sech}(x) (1 - \text{sech}^2(x)) \, dx
\]

This can be split into two integrals:

\[
\int \text{sech}^3(x) \, dx = \text{sech}(x) \tanh(x) + \int \text{sech}(x) \, dx - \int \text{sech}^3(x) \, dx
\]

Let \(I = \int \text{sech}^3(x) \, dx\). Then:

\[
I = \text{sech}(x) \tanh(x) + \int \text{sech}(x) \, dx - I
\]

Adding \(I\) to both sides:

\[
2I = \text{sech}(x) \tanh(x) + \int \text{sech}(x) \, dx
\]

We know that \(\int \text{sech}(x) \, dx = 2 \arctan(e^x) + C\). Thus:

\[
2I = \text{sech}(x) \tanh(x) + 2 \arctan(e^x) + C
\]

Dividing by 2:

\[
I = \frac{1}{2} \text{sech}(x) \tanh(x) + \arctan(e^x) + C
\]

Therefore:

\[
\int \text{sech}^3(x) \, dx = \frac{1}{2} \text{sech}(x) \tanh(x) + \arctan(e^x) + C
\]

Returning to the original integral:

\[
\int \frac{dx}{(e^x + e^{-x})^3} = \frac{1}{8} \left( \frac{1}{2} \text{sech}(x) \tanh(x) + \arctan(e^x) \right) + C
\]

Converting back to exponential form:

\[
\text{sech}(x) = \frac{2}{e^x + e^{-x}} \quad \text{and} \quad \tanh(x) = \frac{e^x - e^{-x}}{e^x + e^{-x}}
\]

Thus:

\[
\text{sech}(x) \tanh(x) = \frac{2(e^x - e^{-x})}{(e^x + e^{-x})^2}
\]

So:

\[
\int \frac{dx}{(e^x + e^{-x})^3} = \frac{1}{8} \left( \frac{1}{2} \cdot \frac{2(e^x - e^{-x})}{(e^x + e^{-x})^2} + \arctan(e^x) \right) + C
\]

Simplifying:

\[
\int \frac{dx}{(e^x + e^{-x})^3} = \frac{e^x - e^{-x}}{8(e^x + e^{-x})^2} + \frac{1}{8} \arctan(e^x) + C
\]

The final answer is:

\[
\boxed{\frac{e^x - e^{-x}}{8(e^x + e^{-x})^2} + \frac{1}{8} \arctan(e^x) + C}
\]<#sep#> the non-R subset's graph are exactly the edges between the non-R points. So to be connected, there must be enough such edges to connect all the non-R nodes. Similarly for the non-B subset.

   163	    @classmethod
   164	    def sanitize(cls, d):
   165	        """
   166	        Identify numbered dictionnaries embeded in the dict and transform them into lists
   167	
   168	        This function is an helper to prevent deprecation after the introduction of pontoon for translation.
   169	        The user is now force to use keys even for numbered lists. SimpleNamespace doesn't support integer indexing
   170	        so this function will transform back this "numbered" dictionnary (with integer keys) into lists.
   171	
   172	        Args:
   173	            d (dict): the dictionnary to sanitize
   174	
   175	        Return:
   176	            (dict): the sanitized dictionnary
   177	        """
   178	
   179	        ms = d.copy()
   180	
   181	        # create generator based on input type
   182	        if isinstance(ms, dict):
   183	            gen = ms.items()
   184	        elif isinstance(ms, list):
   185	            gen = enumerate(ms)
   186	
   187	        # loop into the keys of the dict modify them
   188	        for k, v in gen:
   189	            if isinstance(v, dict):
   190	                tmp = v
   191	                if all([k.isnumeric() for k in tmp]):
   192	                    tmp = list(tmp.values())
   193	                ms[k] = cls.sanitize(tmp)
   194	            else:
   195	                ms[k] = v
   196	
   197	        return ms
   198	
   199	    def _update(self, d, u):
   200	        """
   201	        Update the fallback dictionnaire (d) values with the keys that exist in the target (u) dictionnaire
   202	
   203	        Args:
   204	            d (dict): The fallback dictionary
   205	            u (dict): the target dctionnary
   206	
   207	        Return:
   208	            ms (dict): The updated dictionnay
   209	        """
   210	
   211	        ms = d.copy()
   212	
   213	        for k, v in u.items():
   214	            if isinstance(v, abc.Mapping):
   215	                ms[k] = self._update(d.get(k, {}), v)
   216	            else:
   217	                ms[k] = v
   218	
   219	        return ms
   220	
   221	    def missing_keys(self):

   223	        this function is intended for developer use only

   225	
   226	        Return:
   227	            (str): the list of missing keys
   228	        """
   229	
   230	        # find all the missing keys

   232	            ddiff = DeepDiff(self.default_dict, self.target_dict)[
   233	                "dictionary_item_removed"
   234	            ]
   235	        except Exception:

   252	    def merge_dict(cls, folder):
   253	        """
   254	        gather all the .json file in the provided l10n folder as 1 single json dict
   255	
   256	        the json dict will be sanitysed and the key will be used as if they were coming from 1 single file.
   257	        be careful with duplication. empty string keys will be removed.
   258	
   259	        Args:
   260	            folder (pathlib.path)
   261	
   262	        Return:
   263	            (dict): the json dict with all the keys
   264	
   265	        """
   266	
   267	        final_json = {}
   268	        for f in folder.glob("*.json"):
   269	            tmp_dict = cls.delete_empty(json.loads(f.read_text()))
   270	            final_json = {**final_json, **cls.sanitize(tmp_dict)}
   271	
   272	        return final_json
   273	
   274	    @versionadded(version="2.8.1")
   275	    @classmethod
   276	    def delete_empty(cls, d):
   277	        """
   278	        Remove empty strings ("") recursively from the dictionaries. This is to prevent untranslated strings from
   279	        Crowdin to be uploaded. The dictionnary must only embed dictionnaries and no lists.
   280	
   281	        Args:
   282	            d (dict): the dictionnary to sanitize
   283	
   284	        Return:
   285	            (dict): the sanitized dictionnary
   286	
   287	        """
   288	        for k, v in list(d.items()):
   289	            if isinstance(v, dict):
   290	                cls.delete_empty(v)
   291	            elif v == "":
   292	                del d[k]
   293	
   294	        return d

import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F
from torch.utils.tensorboard import SummaryWriter


def singleton(cls):
    obj = cls()
    cls.__new__ = staticmethod(lambda cls: obj)
    try:
        del cls.__init__
    except AttributeError:
        pass
    return cls


@singleton
class model_debugger:

    def __init__(self):
        self.reset()

    def reset(self, filename_suffix=''):
        """
		Reset all the variables

		Parameters
		----------
		filename_suffix : str, optional
			the filename suffix, by default ""

		Returns
		-------
		model_debugger
			returns self
		"""
        self.model_forward_data = {}
        self.active_forwards = {}
        self.logs = []
        self.time_usage = {}
        self.active_timers = {}
        self.called_times = {}
        self.variable_log = {}
        self.tenserboard_writer = SummaryWriter(filename_suffix)
        self.epoch_write_counter = {}
        return self

    def write_to_tensorboard(self, name, value, epoch):
        """
		Add value to SummaryWriter

		Parameters
		----------
		name : str
			tag name
		value : number
			numeric value
		epoch : epoch
			Current epoch, if none a built in counter will be used
		"""
        if epoch is None:
            if name not in self.epoch_write_counter:
                self.epoch_write_counter[name] = 0
            epoch = self.epoch_write_counter[name]
            self.epoch_write_counter[name] += 1
        self.tenserboard_writer.add_scalar(name, value, epoch)

    def save_tensorboard(self):
        self.tenserboard_writer.close()

    def get_last_round(self):
        """
		Get the latest forward data for model

		Returns
		-------
		list
			with tuple of key value
		"""
        return self.model_forward_data.items()

    def start_forward(self, name):
        """
		Start a forward of component

		Parameters
		----------
		name : str
			name of the component
		"""
        if name not in self.active_forwards:
            self.active_forwards[name] = []

    def add_element(self, name, element):
        """
		Add a element to the forward path of a given name

		Parameters
		----------
		name : str
			name of component
		element : tensor/any
			the tensor of a given state
		"""
        self.active_forwards[name].append(element)

    def stop_forward(self, name):
        """
		Complete a forward

		Parameters
		----------
		name : str
			name of the component
		"""
        self.model_forward_data[name] = self.active_forwards[name]
        self.active_forwards[name] = []

    def log(self, value):
        """
		Log a value to list

		Parameters
		----------
		value : str
			log value
		"""
        self.logs.append(value)

    def start_track_time(self, name):
        """
		Track time usage of a given component
		- need to be closed by stop_track_time

		Parameters
		----------
		name : str
			name of timer
		"""
        if name not in self.active_timers:
            self.active_timers[name] = []
            self.time_usage[name] = 0
            self.called_times[name] = 0
        self.active_timers[name].append(time.time())
        self.called_times[name] += 1

    def stop_track_time(self, name):
        """
		Stops the timer with a given name

		Parameters
		----------
		name : str
			name of timer
		"""
        time_usage = time.time() - self.active_timers[name].pop(0)
        self.time_usage[name] += time_usage


class shared_backbone:

    def __init__(self):
        self.device = torch.device('cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else
            'cpu')
        self.debugger = model_debugger()
        self.main_hidden_layer_size = 64


class BasicConv(nn.Module, shared_backbone):

    def __init__(self, output=120):
        super().__init__()
        shared_backbone.__init__(self)
        self.output_size = 96
        self.conv1 = nn.Conv2d(1, 6, 3)
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(self.output_size, output)

    def forward(self, x):
        """
		Basic forward operation on conv layer

		Parameters
		----------
		x : torch.tensor
			input data

		Returns
		-------
		torch.tensor
			the output after the operations
		"""
        if len(x.shape) < 4:
            padding = (1,) * (4 - len(x.shape))
            x = x.reshape(padding + x.shape)
        x = F.max_pool2d(F.relu(self.conv1(x)), (2, 2))
        x = x.view(-1, self.output_size)
        assert x.shape[0] == 1
        assert len(x.shape) == 2
        return F.relu(self.fc1(x))


def get_inputs():
    return [torch.rand([4, 1, 6, 6])]


def get_init_inputs():
    return [[], {}]
